Calcula los indices escalados por la entrada global 0x80116aa0 y por a0, forma la direccion base 0x80118130 y escribe cero, a1 y cero en los halfwords +0xab6/+0xab8/+0xab4. La validacion byte-match contra el dump licito SLPM_868.97_1 coincidio 124/124 bytes en el offset 0x30a4c, con SHA-256 de referencia y construido a1b07301c970895f1d633e9b8554271f97a0970f7fb6c385f3aff1ca20ead03a, usando GCC 14.2.0/binutils 2.43.

La funcion recorre lhu +4 elementos desde a0+8, suma a0 a cada word y devuelve 1; su llamador FUN_8007dfbc ya cuenta con coincidencia verificada. ## 2026-08-04 (9)
* **Eighth confirmed PsyQ object boundary (`PADCMD`), a third consecutive object in the PAD cluster**: `PADCMD` (`0x8003d7d8`–`0x8003e3d4`, 3,068 bytes, 42 rows) sits immediately after `PADMAIN`, completing a three-in-a-row confirmed sequence (`PADENTRY` → `PADMAIN` → `PADCMD`). Complete byte account, with the same kind of caveat as `PADENTRY` stated plainly: three 4-byte fragments (`PADCMD_OBJ_2FC`, `PADCMD_OBJ_8B0`, `PADCMD_OBJ_B40`) are `confidence: library_signature`, not yet individually byte-matched. Base occupied directly by the independently byte-matched `_padSetAct`; measured end lands exactly on the independently byte-matched `FUN_8003e3d4`. Leading edge shows a real 12-byte zero-padding run after `_padWaitRXready` (the same shape `PADMAIN`'s own leading edge showed, a separate instance not a re-measurement); trailing edge is dense, directly onto `FUN_8003e3d4`'s own first (real, byte-matched) instruction. All four criteria met: eighth `object_boundary_confirmed` result. Updated `linked-object-evidence.md`'s scope statement and bar-definition conclusion in place, and README's target-2 status.

## 2026-08-04 (8)
* **Seventh confirmed PsyQ object boundary (`PADMAIN`), adjacent to `PADENTRY`**: `PADMAIN` (`0x8003ca08`–`0x8003d714`, 3,340 bytes, 23 rows, all `confidence: verified` with no caveats: `PadEnableCom`, `_padSetVsyncParam`, `_padChkVsync`, `_padStartCom`, `_padStopCom`, `_padInitSioMode`, `_padSioRW`, `_padSioRW2`, the gap-sweep-reconstructed `FUN_8003d154`, and fourteen `PADMAIN_OBJ_*` fragments) sits immediately after `PADENTRY` in the same PAD-driver cluster, letting the shared seam be checked from both sides. Complete byte account; base occupied directly by the independently byte-matched `PadEnableCom` (the `SYS`/`ResetGraph` base-occupancy pattern). The leading edge is not dense: `PadSetAct` ends at `0x8003c9fc`, followed by a real 12-byte zero-padding run up to the base `0x8003ca08` — a distinct gap from `PADENTRY`'s own (different) padding finding, not a duplicate. The trailing edge is dense, landing directly on `_padClrIntSio0`. All four criteria met: seventh `object_boundary_confirmed` result. Simplified README's per-object listing (now summarized by name/count rather than repeating full addresses inline, since the authoritative detail lives in `linked-object-evidence.md`) to keep it from growing unboundedly with each new confirmation. Updated `linked-object-evidence.md`'s scope statement and bar-definition conclusion in place.

## 2026-08-04 (7)
* **Sixth confirmed PsyQ object boundary (`PADENTRY`), from the PAD-driver cluster**: Picked a candidate from a third distinct library region (PAD/controller driver, not SPU or `GS_*` graphics) to keep testing the method against different parts of the executable. `PADENTRY` (`0x8003c548`–`0x8003c934`, 1,004 bytes, 20 rows: `PadChkVsync`, `PadStartCom`, `PadStopCom`, `PadChkMtap`, `PadGetState`, `PadInfoMode`, `PadInfoAct`, `PadInfoComb`, the gap-sweep-reconstructed `FUN_8003c874`, and eleven `PADENTRY_OBJ_*`/named fragments) has a complete byte account — with one honest caveat carried forward rather than smoothed over: one interior row, `PADENTRY_OBJ_164` (4 bytes), is `confidence: library_signature`, not yet individually byte-matched, only signature-identified; its bytes are still accounted for in the span, just not yet exact-matched like every other row here. The object's base is occupied directly by the independently byte-matched `PadChkVsync` (the same base-occupancy pattern as `SYS`/`ResetGraph`), and the measured end lands exactly on the independently byte-matched `PadSetActAlign`'s start. The true leading edge is **not** dense: `_remove_ChgclrPAD` ends at `0x8003c540`, followed by a real 8-byte zero-padding run up to the base at `0x8003c548`; the trailing edge is dense. All four criteria met (padded-leading/dense-trailing, the same shape `FORMAT` showed): sixth `object_boundary_confirmed` result, and the first found outside the SPU-library and `GS_*`-graphics regions. Updated `linked-object-evidence.md`'s scope statement and bar-definition conclusion in place, and README's target-2 status and Completed foundations.

## 2026-08-04 (6)
* **Fifth confirmed PsyQ object boundary (`PRESET`); third dense-edge negative result (`VSYNC`)**: Checked the remaining strongest double/triple-corroborated candidates from the broadened scan. `VSYNC` (`0x800358f8`–`0x80035b08`, 528 bytes, 4 rows) has the strongest criterion-2 signal found yet — a *triple* corroboration (`VSync` at its base, `_SN_read` immediately before, `ResetCallback` immediately after, all independently byte-matched) and a complete byte account, but both true edges turned out dense with zero padding — a third negative result, joining `BIOS_OBJ_*` and `UT_REV`. All three dense-edge negatives sit inside the same tightly packed SPU/sequencer-library region (`0x8002d9bc`–`0x80035xxx`); tentatively (not proven) this whole region may be one dense object rather than many small ones with padding at every internal boundary. Testing that idea, `PRESET` (`0x8007c4e0`–`0x8007ce44`, 2,404 bytes, 23 rows) was picked from a different region — the `GS_*` graphics library area — and found to have a complete byte account, a dense leading edge landing exactly on the independently byte-matched `GsMapModelingData`'s end, and a real 12-byte zero-padding trailing edge. One caveat recorded plainly: the row immediately after that padding (`FUN_8007ce50`) is currently `unverified`, so this edge's criterion-2 corroboration comes from the *opposite* (leading) edge rather than sitting alongside the padding itself, unlike the prior four confirmations. All four criteria still met: fifth `object_boundary_confirmed` result. New instruction classes appeared at this scale: `lwc2`/`swc2`/`cfc2` (GTE data transfer) and raw `c2 0xNNNNNN` GTE macro opcodes, both of which GNU `as` accepts directly in the exact form GNU objdump's `-M no-aliases` prints them, needing no special handling. First verification pass: 281/297 passed; the 16 failures all shared one root cause, found and fixed: every failing function contained a `j` to a *local* label (a target within the function's own address range) with no manifest `symbols` entry, so `Invoke-FunctionMatch.ps1` took the unlinked direct-objcopy path, and GNU `as` encodes an absolute J-type jump target as if the section's base address were `0x0` rather than its real load address unless the linker places it — silently producing a wrong-but-plausible-looking absolute target. Adding an empty `"symbols": {}` to each affected manifest entry (sufficient to force the linked-placement code path per `Invoke-FunctionMatch.ps1`'s existing property-presence check) fixed all 16 with no source changes. All 297 functions matched thereafter; none of the 12 rows flagged with a less-certain upper bound needed a boundary correction — their declared sizes were already correct. All 297 manifest entries carry equal reference/built SHA-256 pairs; all 297 CSV rows promoted to `confidence = verified`/`source_status = hand_written_source` with per-row durable evidence. * **Library signature reconstruction batch 4 — candidate delivered after external delegation failure**: The external agent run delegated to execute `docs/workflows/external-agent-library-signature-batch4.md` (fixed base `05cd68c`) failed mid-task, leaving an isolated worktree with a complete but unverified 82-function assembly source and manifest entries lacking hashes, never committed or pushed. Continued the package directly from that worktree rather than re-delegating. Running `Invoke-FunctionMatch.ps1` on all 82 owned functions found 11 mismatches and traced both root causes: ten functions used the assembler's `move` pseudo-instruction, which GNU binutils 2.43 always expands to `or $rd,$rs,$zero` (funct `0x25`), while the lawful binary requires the explicit `addu $rd,$rs,$zero` (funct `0x21`) form; the eleventh (`SYS_OBJ_181C`) had a transcribed `lw` immediate of `-0x72bc` where raw executable bytes confirmed `-0x727c`. After correcting both defect classes in `src/ddr5thmix/LibrarySignatureBatch4.s`, all 82/82 functions matched with equal reference/built SHA-256 pairs, recorded in the manifest, and all 82 CSV rows were promoted to `confidence = verified`/`source_status = hand_written_source` with per-row durable evidence (command, lawful executable hash, toolchain versions, byte count, matching hash). `screen-selector`, `mode-control`, and `runtime-core` module regressions still pass. Main-executable totals move from 367 to 449 verified functions (17.3% to 21.1%), `library_signature` from 778 to 696, `decompiler_output_only` from 1,658 to 1,576, and exact main-executable bytes from 19,364 to 21,468 (519 functions / 30,840 selected bytes overall). Delivered to `agent/library-signature-reconstruction-batch4`; remains `candidate_for_audit` pending a separate clean-checkout audit per `docs/workflows/external-agent-acceptance.md`.

* **Inst-demo-overlay 80-byte range, third pass: dynamic (BizHawk) verification, write-negative confirmed, no classification change**: Static analysis for the remaining 80 unresolved bytes (two 24-byte all-zero runs plus a partially-referenced 32-byte table) was credibly exhausted, so this pass tried running the actual game in BizHawk 2.11 (PSX core `Nymashock`, Waterbox/Nyma-wrapped) with no controller input through the documented attract loop, watching the three ranges while HOW TO PLAY plays. Reaching HOW TO PLAY required dismissing one real, documented boot-time dialog pair (BizHawk mounts a blank virtual memory card; the game detects "card present, no system data" exactly as the screen-flow doc's "Memory Card Auto Load" step describes) with a single `○` press each — not a synthetic skip — after which the title screen's own documented 900-frame inactivity timeout returns to the attract loop's `WARNING` state on its own; every frame from there through HOW TO PLAY is genuinely zero-input. The active window was confirmed both by polling the documented screen-index global `DAT_800f2908` and, independently, by a mid-window screenshot literally showing the "HOW TO PLAY" tutorial screen; its measured length (1,989 frames) is close to but not identical to the 1,910-tick script trace already verified statically. BizHawk's `event.on_bus_read`/`on_bus_write`/`on_bus_exec` bus-hook API was tested and found **non-functional for this core/session** (`event.availableScopes()` returns zero scopes; a control hook on a function documented as firing every active frame never fired once) — a tooling limitation, not a game claim, now recorded in `docs/tooling/bizhawk-harness.md` alongside an empirically-found BizHawk input-timing fact (simulated presses held only 1-2 frames can be silently dropped on a lag frame; hold ~10 frames) and the real (unprefixed, glyph-named) PSX joypad button names this core exposes to Lua. The working fallback, plain per-frame `memory.read_u8` polling/diffing (confirmed functional via the working screen-index poll), found **zero byte-value changes in all three target ranges across the full visually-confirmed HOW TO PLAY window** — a genuine dynamic negative result for writes specifically, on top of the two existing independent static zero-reference sweeps for the two all-zero runs; it cannot detect reads with no side effect, so it neither confirms nor refutes the partially-referenced table's single ambiguous static read. No range map CSV classification changed (all three sub-ranges remain `data_unresolved`/`unverified`): a third independent negative result does not by itself constitute a resolved consumer, per this project's evidentiary standard. Added `tools/bizhawk/probe-inst-demo-watch.lua` and a `-LuaPath` parameter on `tools/bizhawk/run-probe.ps1`; documented method, findings, and reproduction in a new "Third pass" section of `docs/games/ddr-5th-mix-jp-inst-demo-overlay.md` and in `docs/tooling/bizhawk-harness.md`. * **External prior-art check (decomp.me): negative result, not added as guidance**: Searched decomp.me, decomp.wiki, `decomp-permuter` and its PsyQ forks, and other public PS1 matching-decompilation projects (sotn-decomp, mkst/esa, FoxdieTeam/mgs_reversing, sozud/psy-q-decomp) for reusable GCC-vs-PsyQ matching techniques. DDR 5th Mix (Japan) is not itself present on decomp.me. decomp.me does list a `psyq4.4` compiler preset, matching this project's already-identified PsyQ SDK version, and confirms `CC1PSX.EXE` is a patched **GCC 2.8.1** fork rather than an unrelated proprietary compiler -- a useful lineage fact explaining why this project's GCC-14-vs-PsyQ mismatches (register allocation, frame/epilogue shape, sibling-call elimination) are expected. However, every known project in that ecosystem matches PsyQ output with **period-correct GCC 2.x** (or the real PsyQ binary), never a modern GCC generation-14 bootstrap like `/config/ddr5thmix/build.json`'s. No precedent was found for this project's specific approach, so none of decomp.me's cataloged quirks, `maspsx` (an ASPSX-equivalence translator built for GCC 2.x output), or the `decomp-permuter-psyq` forks (noted incomplete: `import.py` reportedly non-functional) are recorded as applicable guidance here -- doing so without independent verification against this project's own GCC 14/binutils 2.43 output would risk misattributing a different compiler generation's behavior. This finding is deliberately not added to `/docs/tooling/toolchain.md`'s recommended stack or citations.

GCC 14.2.0/binutils 2.43 matches all 3,356 selected bytes across all twelve functions, every one on its first reconstruction attempt; no compiler-compatibility shim was needed since the module is written directly as semantic MIPS assembly, mirroring `GameSessionOpening.s`/`GameSessionRouter.s`. Promoted seven reviewed helpers; totals remain 2,118 functions / 190 manual rows / 509,608 function-body bytes.
* **Attract-loop runtime order correction**: Reviewed 11 consecutively named owner-supplied captures and made their temporal order authoritative: `WARNING -> KONAMI -> BEMANI -> Dancemania/intercord japan/TOSHIBA EMI promotion -> DDR 5thMIX title -> HOW TO PLAY -> DEMONSTRATION -> BEST RANKING -> WARNING`. The last capture repeats the first and proves that WARNING, not RANKING or Company, is the visible loop boundary. Rotated the already-confirmed seven-state cyclic graph to `WARNING(0) -> TITLE(2) -> PLAY DEMO(5) -> CATCH DEMO(3) -> CATCH DEMO(4) -> RANKING(6) -> WARNING(0)` and separated `FUN_80053ed8`'s conditional constructor choice from observed presentation order. The images remain untracked copyrighted output; exact substate boundaries for intermediate logo, promotional, title, and HOW TO PLAY changes remain open.
* **PLAY DEMO/terminal correction**: Exhaustively audited all 15 session-child updates and the seven-state attract child. Only GAME_OVER state 9 and ENDING state 10 can naturally return terminal 14; their exits set `0x800100ff` before outer dispatch, while the force path requires it already set. Thus a valid player session always follows outer 10/11 to the menu and cannot reach outer 0. Corrected the earlier index error: outer 0 enters `0x25` `RANKING`, not `PLAY DEMO`; true `PLAY DEMO` is attract state 5/index `0x24`, inside the confirmed `RANKING -> WARNING -> TITLE -> PLAY DEMO -> CATCH DEMO -> RANKING` cycle. TITLE loads a `0x2e58`-byte `inst demo` executable overlay from `READ_DT.BIN` LBA `0x7a80` to `0x801e4000`; its callbacks use 3D lighting, proving automated rendered gameplay rather than video, while literal reuse of DANCING judgment/scoring remains pending overlay import. Also corrected child state 10 from inherited LINK END to direct screen index `0x15` `ENDING`. Added 26 exact callback boundaries; map totals are 2,118 functions / 183 manual rows / 509,608 bytes.
* **Outer session closure (corrected above)**: Traced the child-terminal handoff and every xref to effective byte `0x800100ff` (`PTR_DAT_800ac8e8[0xff]`). The byte is operationally a force-terminal/destination latch: zero permits outer state 0, while nonzero forces child state 14 and selects outer 10. Outer 10 runs a 42-update radial 4x3-cell transition, outer 11 restores a 512x480/640x480 pipeline, then returns to outer 1's title/PUSH START main menu. GAME_OVER/ENDING exits set the latch before outer dispatch and therefore take the menu branch; a long-Cross-hold detector can also set it, while main-menu entry or whole-block initialization clears it. Added six exact indirect callback boundaries, promoted eight fully reviewed helpers, and updated the map to 2,092 functions / 157 manual rows / 506,364 covered bytes. The newer entry corrects the old `0x25=PLAY DEMO` label to `RANKING` and proves outer 0 unreachable from a valid session.
* **Result/end-session graph**: Used parallel, non-overlapping reviews of the 15-state game-session child to close every branch after `STAGE END`. State 8 is `RESULT` and returns either to selector-owner state 1 or `PRE_END`; state 11 is `PRE_END` and returns to state 1 or advances to state 12 `LINK END`; `LINK END` branches to state 9 `GAME_OVER`, state 13 `NAME ENTRY`, or a null-entry wait state 10. `GAME_OVER` and state 10 converge on terminal 14; `NAME ENTRY` first passes through state 10. The selector return enters `MUSIC SEL` only under the exact `FUN_80075580` condition, otherwise it restarts at `STYLE SEL`. Also reviewed `FUN_8007fdec` as a per-player timeline classifier (`-1` prestart, `0` active, `1`/`2` normal terminal, `-2` special terminal without overclaiming "failure"). Added 17 authentic indirect boundaries, promoted the classifier, verified removal of three exploratory internal boundaries and restoration of `FUN_8006fa30`/`FUN_8006fba4`; map totals are now 2,086 functions, 143 manual rows, and 506,160 covered bytes.
* **Gameplay-state route**: Continued the confirmed `GAME MODE` path past `MUSIC SEL` through the 15-state child. The selector terminates into state 5, whose screen index 1 is `PREPARE` (correcting an earlier off-by-one `INTRO` label), then state 4 writes index 2 `INTRO`. `INTRO` can route to state 6 or 7; state 6's entry writes index 4 `DANCING`, its update remains there during play and exits toward state 7 on completion, and its exit computes per-player result values. State 7 writes index 5 `STAGE END`, performs timed transition work, and accumulates result/stat fields before branching onward. Added and manually reviewed the nine indirect callbacks for states 4, 6, and 7; the map now has 2,069 functions / 125 manual rows and 504,344 bytes of function-body coverage.
* **State-2 selector map**: Followed the main menu's confirmed `GAME MODE`/`EVENT MODE -> DAT_80105124 state 2` target through three layers of enter/update/exit tables. Outer state 2 is `FUN_8004bdec`/`FUN_8004b800`/`FUN_8004be30`; it owns a 15-state game-session child, whose state 1 owns a six-state screen selector. Entry callbacks provide exact screen-index identities: substate 0 `STYLE SEL` (`FUN_800756f0`), 1 `MODE SEL` (`FUN_800757e0`), 2 `CHARA SEL` (`FUN_80075840`), 3 `MUSIC SEL` (`FUN_800754b4`), 4 `LINK START` (`FUN_80075a40`), and 5 terminal. `FUN_80075af8` confirms `STYLE -> LINK START -> CHARA -> MUSIC -> terminal`, with `LINK START -> MUSIC` as the character-select skip path controlled by `PTR_DAT_800e0b18[0x98]`; no normal incoming route to `MODE SEL` was found. Added 26 indirect callback boundaries, promoted 8 existing wrappers/routers, and updated the symbol map to 2,060 functions / 116 manual rows.
* **Runtime screen corroboration**: Reviewed the owner's expanded, untracked screenshot set. `MainMenu.png` is the renamed original main-menu capture. Following its rows produces screens visibly headed `ARCADE LINK`, `RECORDS` (including a `NO MUSIC` empty state), `OPTION`, `GALLERY MODE`, and `INFORMATION`, confirming the already-derived outer-state identities 6, 7, 8, 13, and 9. Three consecutive `GAME MODE` captures independently confirm the visible setup order `SELECT STYLE -> SELECT CHARACTER -> SELECT MUSIC`, matching the internal `STYLE SEL`/`CHARA SEL`/`MUSIC SEL` strings; this identifies state 2 as entry to that flow but does not yet assign numeric values to its nested screen states.
* **Music-database correction + export**: The literal `data/mdb/mdb.bin` is not an ISO 9660 entry, and reviewing its call target refutes the earlier loader interpretation: `FUN_8007eea8` is exactly `jr ra; nop`, so the path is a retained source/build marker with no runtime file-open behavior. Located the runtime metadata already linked into `SLPM_868.97`: 47 records at `DAT_800df3d8` (stride `0x1c`), a separate 50-ID table at `DAT_800df900`, and title strings. Added a hash-gated exporter plus a 47-row CSV with music IDs, titles, SP/DP difficulty nibbles, unlock selectors, BPM pairs, and preserved unknown fields. Also added a recursive ISO extractor, recorded the negative `READ_DT.BIN` container result, and corrected the screen-name string start from the marker's `0x8001bb10` to `0x8001bb24`.
* **Main-menu identification**: Reviewed `DAT_80105124` state 1's enter/update/exit callbacks and its selector/render helpers, then matched them against the repository owner's runtime screenshot (kept untracked as copyrighted game output). State 1 is the 11-row main menu on the title/`PUSH START` screen: it loads `title_25`/`hbota_25`, navigates with Up/Down, confirms with Start/Circle, and returns to state 0 after 900 inactive frames; the owner independently confirms that reaching this timeout returns to the Attract Loop. Recorded the exact destination-state mapping for all 11 visible options; `GAME MODE` and `EVENT MODE` converge on state 2 with different setup flags. Added 3 indirect callback boundaries and promoted 12 existing helpers after manual review (2,034 total functions, 71 manual).
* **Input tracing + tooling**: Traced every reference to `DAT_800e3b60` and `DAT_800e3b70` with new `DumpDataXrefs.java`, which prints exact reference type/function/instruction: `FUN_8002112c` is the sole writer of both. It runs once per frame, validates two PsyQ pad receive buffers, converts their active-low button bytes, and publishes previous/current/newly-pressed/newly-released words; these two globals are specifically newly pressed for ports 1/2. Their buffers are registered during `FUN_80021dfc` through real PsyQ `PadInitDirect(0x800e2a68,0x800e2a9c)` + `PadStartCom`, so the low-level driver is PsyQ/BIOS and `FUN_8002112c` is the game-side adapter. Packet order confirms `0x800=Start`, `0x100=Select`, `0x20=Circle`: `FUN_8004b554` advances on Start or Circle newly pressed, and `FUN_80021374` is now statically confirmed as Start-held + Select-newly-pressed return-to-attract, not merely a structural match.
* **Review**: Reviewed `DAT_80105124`'s state-0 enter/update/exit triple, starting at requested target `FUN_8004bcc8`. It does not recurse into a third copy of the same generic 14-state machine; instead it owns a distinct subordinate 7-state enter/update/exit dispatcher at tables `0x800ddc68`/`0x800ddc84`/`0x800ddca0`, normally initialized to state 6 (state 0 only for `DAT_800f2908` values `0x1c`/`0x1d`). The outer update ticks that child until mask `0x820` appears in either of two input words, then returns `1`, confirming an outer transition `0 -> 1` without yet naming the buttons or screen. `DumpFunctionDetail.java` created three missing indirect callback boundaries; the symbol map now has 2,031 functions and 54 manually reviewed rows.
* **Correction**: The repository owner describes a real Consumer-build feature -- pressing Start+Select during the game loop immediately returns to the `KONAMI`/`BEMANI`/`TOSHIBA` attract screens. This is a strong structural match for `FUN_80021374`'s button-combo-triggered transition to mode `0xff` (a reset/service state performing a GPU reset and re-arming `main`'s loop-restart flag), identified 2026-07-14. This withdraws `FUN_80021374` as evidence for the "arcade-only leftover" quirk entry -- it's better explained as a legitimate, intentional feature, not an inaccessible arcade leftover. The quirk entry, symbol map, and screen-flow doc all corrected; the entry's other evidence (the `TEST_MODE` string and mode `0x10`'s still-unreachable menu) stands on its own.
* **Screen flow**: Recorded the repository owner's domain-knowledge account of the actual gameplay session flow on Consumer BEMANI releases (distinct from the attract loop already recorded): Caution -> Style Select -> Select Character -> Select Music -> Gameplay -> Result screen -> (Select Music / Summary / Game Over depending on settings). Maps directly onto more of the 42-name string table: `WARNING`=Caution, `STYLE SEL`=Style Select, `CHARA SEL`=Select Character, `MUSIC SEL`=Select Music, `DANCING`=Gameplay, `RESULT`=Result, `GAME_OVER`=Game Over. No string obviously matches "Summary" yet (`ENDING`/`PRE_END`/`SELECT END` are candidates, unconfirmed).
* **Correction**: Read one level deeper into `DAT_80105120` state 0 (`FUN_8004ba34`/`FUN_8004bbb4`/`FUN_8004bc54`), correcting the same-day "45 entries = 15 states" reading. `FUN_8004ba34` bootstraps a *second, independent, nested* state machine on a different global (`DAT_80105120+4`), and `FUN_8004bbb4`/`FUN_8004bc54` turn out to be a generic, reusable, parameterized version of `FUN_80049dec`'s own tick logic -- confirming the engine has one reusable "tick a state machine" primitive used both at the top level and recursively for nested children. Reading the nested child's own 3 tables directly (`tools/ghidra/scripts/DumpJumpTable.java` against `0x800d9ac8`) confirmed it has **14 states** (3 flat 14-entry arrays, not interleaved triples) -- so the 45 raw entries are actually `DAT_80105120`'s own 1-state (3-word) table immediately followed by this separate 14-state (42-word) child table, not one 15-state table. `DAT_80105120` itself only ever holds state 0 in every call site found so far; the real branching is one level down. Neither 15 nor 14 matches the 42-name string table's count, so that correction stands regardless.
* **Tooling + review**: Extended `tools/ghidra/scripts/DumpFunctionDetail.java` to create a function boundary (`CreateFunctionCmd`) when asked to dump an address Ghidra's auto-analysis never turned into a function -- needed for `DAT_80105120`'s update/exit callbacks, which are only reached indirectly. Reviewed state 0's enter/update/exit triple (`FUN_80049c24`/`FUN_80049f7c`/`FUN_80049fa4`): `FUN_80049c24` (enter) is called directly by `FUN_80049d3c` as part of mode `0x04`'s boot chain, confirming the state machine is explicitly bootstrapped there; it shares inline player-record init code with `FUN_80049d3c`. `FUN_80049f7c` (update) always returns a literal 0, meaning it never itself triggers a transition. Neither reveals a screen identity yet -- the real logic is one level deeper, in unreviewed `FUN_8004ba34`/`FUN_8004bbb4`/`FUN_8004bc54`. Added 2 new symbol-map rows for the two previously-function-less addresses (2,028 total functions now, up from 2,026).
* **Quirks**: Added the DDR 5th Mix quirk log's first entry, "Arcade-only functionality left in the Consumer build, unreachable but not removed" (`status: suspected_shared`), per the repository owner's domain knowledge that BEMANI PS1 ports routinely leave arcade-specific service/test/debug code compiled in without a reachable retail-control trigger, rather than stripped. This gives a coherent explanation for two open puzzles: `FUN_80021374`'s arcade-test-button-shaped service/reset-combo watcher, and why no path has been found into mode `0x10`'s interactive menu (`FUN_80022b30`) despite an exhaustive search -- "no path found" may be the correct final answer, not an unsolved gap.
* **Correction**: Read the raw contents of `DAT_80105120`'s three function-pointer tables (`tools/ghidra/scripts/DumpJumpTable.java` against `0x800d9abc`) and found exactly 45 valid code-address entries (`45 = 15 x 3`, an enter/update/exit triple for 15 states), terminated by `0xFFFFFFFF` -- not 42. This refutes the earlier same-day claim that `DAT_80105120` directly indexes the 42-entry screen-name string table (15 != 42); the two are more likely separate structures. The string table's own contents remain valid evidence for the screen names it lists -- only the "what indexes it" claim is withdrawn. A more conservative reading is that the 42-name table is a debug/test-mode menu listing rather than the engine's live state enum. Bonus: found a further, unexplored table of option/settings-name strings ("Game Mode", "Diet Mode") immediately after the 45-entry table's terminator.
* **Discovery**: Found a 42-entry screen-name string table at `0x8001bb10` (`BOOT`, `PREPARE`, `INTRO`, `HOW TO`, `DANCING`, `STAGE END`, `RESULT`, `NON STOP I/C`, `STYLE SEL`, `MODE SEL`, `CHARA SEL`, `MUSIC SEL`, `SEQKIND SEL`, `LINK SEL`, `COURSE SEL`, `INRAN SEL`, `EDSEQ_SEL`, `SELECT END`, `NAME ENTRY`, `PRE_END`, `ENDING`, `URL&PASS`, `LINK START/END`, `GAME_OVER`, `PLAY START`, `GAME ??`, `PUSH START`, `WARNING`, `KONAMI`, `BEMANI`, `TOSHIBA`, `TMOVIE`, `TITLE`, `CATCH DEMO`, `PLAY DEMO`, `RANKING`, `LETS LINK`, `DEMO ??`, `TEST_MODE`, `OTHER`) while chasing `FUN_80049dec`'s `"TEST_MODE"` string, using a new tool, `tools/ghidra/scripts/DumpBytes.java` (raw hex+ASCII dump). This is almost certainly the game's real top-level screen enum, indexed by `DAT_80105120` through three parallel function-pointer tables (update/exit/enter) -- a much richer state machine than the `PTR_DAT_800ac8e8` mode/submode system this document has focused on so far. Directly confirms the repository owner's domain-knowledge account with literal string evidence: `WARNING`=Caution, `KONAMI`/`BEMANI`/`TOSHIBA`=three separate Company logo screens, `HOW TO`=How To Play, `PLAY DEMO`=Gameplay Demonstration, `RANKING`=Ranking. One confirmed link to the existing mode system: mode `0x04` initializes `DAT_80105120` to state 0 (`BOOT`); mode `0x02`/submode `0x02` pumps it every frame. How the two systems fully relate is not yet established -- likely the next major thread, possibly meriting its own `DAT_80105120`-keyed screen-flow document.
* **Review**: Reviewed mode `0x02`'s three submode handlers, prompted by the repository owner recalling (from prior BizHawk observation) that DDR 5th Mix switches display resolutions during play. Confirmed directly: submodes `0x00`/`0x01` (`FUN_8009f820`/`FUN_8009f390`) are a display-off/resize-to-640x480/delay/display-on resolution-change sequence -- matching the owner's runtime observation exactly. Submode `0x02` (`FUN_80049dec`) turned out unrelated -- a separate enter/exit/update state machine keyed on `DAT_80105120` (shared with mode `0x04`'s music-database loader) that copies a table ending at an entry containing a pointer to the literal string `"TEST_MODE"`, this project's first human-readable string. Every mode reviewed in the `0x00 -> 0x10 -> 0x04 -> 0x02` chain now reads as infrastructure (memory card I/O, GPU reset, asset/database loading, resolution switching), not a visible screen -- the first real attract-loop screen identity is still further down the chain, not yet located.
* **Review**: Reviewed mode `0x04`'s remaining 6 callees, completing its call tree. Found this project's first literal filename string, `"data/mdb/mdb.bin"` (loaded by `FUN_80049d3c`, called from both of mode `0x04`'s handlers) -- almost certainly the song/chart metadata database. Also found real PsyQ SPU/reverb setup calls (`FUN_8002a7a4`) and a settings-clamp routine sanitizing ~25 option bytes of a third struct (`FUN_8009b0a8`, `PTR_DAT_800e0b18`). This reframes the whole `0x00 -> 0x10 -> 0x04` chain as boot/loading infrastructure (memory card load -> GPU reset + asset load -> settings validation + music DB load + audio setup) rather than a sequence of visible screens, pushing the search for the first real screen identity to mode `2`'s own submodes. Also corrected an ambiguous reading of `FUN_8002340c`'s two branches: only one (`PTR_DAT_800ac8ec[6] != 0`) is an actual mode transition; the other advances submode within mode `0x04`.
* **Review**: Reviewed `FUN_800232cc`'s (mode `0x10`/submode `0x00`) two callees, `FUN_800222fc` and `FUN_8002a9dc`. Neither touches submode (the "does the menu get armed during boot" question stays open), but both clarify what this submode is for: `FUN_800222fc` is pure GPU reinitialization built from real PsyQ calls (`ResetGraph`/`GsInitGraph2`/`GsInit3D`/`InitGeom`/`ClearImage2`/`DrawSync`); `FUN_8002a9dc` (called from 22 places project-wide, confirming it's generic) is an asset loader that draws primitives then uploads images to VRAM via the real `LoadImage` call. This reframes mode `0x10`/submode `0x00` as generic screen-entry boilerplate -- reset GPU, load assets, hand off to mode `4` -- rather than a screen in its own right, nudging the "title/mode-select" guess toward mode `4` instead of mode `0x10` itself.

## 2026-07-14
* **Review**: Tested the repository owner's hypothesis that mode `0x10`'s menu might be armed during Memory Card Auto Load (mode `0x00`), explaining why it's ready the instant Start is pressed. Not confirmed: enumerating every write to `+0x2a` (submode) across all 58 functions referencing `PTR_DAT_800ac8e8` found only three writers -- `FUN_80023210` (`SetMode`, resets submode to 0), newly-identified `FUN_800231b0` (`NextSubmode`, increments submode -- explains a function called from nearly every handler reviewed so far), and `FUN_80023230` (`SetSubmode`, sets a literal value, only called once to advance mode `0x00`'s own local sequence). No path was found that leaves mode `0x10`'s submode at `1` across a frame boundary -- a genuine, well-supported open puzzle now, not a review gap. Side-discovery: `FUN_80021374` (previously an unexplained second mode-field reader) is a service/reset-combo watcher that transitions to mode `0xff` on a specific controller-button combo, explaining that mode's elaborate 5-way dispatch as a real reset/service state.
* **Review**: Reviewed mode `0x10`'s two submode handlers to test the "is mode 0x10 the Company screen?" hypothesis from earlier the same day. Refuted it: `FUN_80022b30` (submode `0x01`) is a real d-pad-cycle-and-confirm menu with a 3-entry destination-mode lookup table (`DAT_800ac8e0 = {4, 0x32, 0x20}`, read directly with a new tool, `tools/ghidra/scripts/DumpShorts.java`), not passive splash-screen code -- a non-interactive attract-loop screen wouldn't need selection logic across 2 controller ports. New hypothesis: mode `0x10` is a title/mode-select hub, `suspected`. This also closes the loop on the confirmed transition graph: mode `0x10` <-> modes `0x20`/`0x32` (menu choices 1/2 send you there, their handlers send you back), while submode `0x00` (`FUN_800232cc`) always unconditionally forwards to mode `4` (which itself flows to mode `2`). `FUN_800232cc` also reaches a second real PsyQ kernel call, `SetDispMask(1)`. New open question: since `SetMode` always resets submode to 0, and submode 0 always forwards away immediately, it's unclear how submode ever advances to 1 to show the menu at all.
* **Tooling + review**: Added `tools/ghidra/scripts/DumpFieldXrefs.java` (dumps decompiled C for every function referencing a given global) and used it on `PTR_DAT_800ac8e8` (58 referencing functions) to chase the long-open "where is the mode field (+0x28) ever written" question. Found `FUN_80023210` is the game's sole `SetMode(newMode)` primitive: writes its argument to +0x28 and clears submode (+0x2a) plus two previously-unseen fields (+0x2c/+0x2e). This turns every already-reviewed call site to it into a confirmed mode transition (modes 0x00/0x20/0x32 -> 0x10; mode 0x04 -> 2; any mode -> 0x80 via the dispatcher's own epilogue flag), resolves that open question, and refutes the earlier +0x17-as-queued-next-mode hypothesis (superseded, not deleted) since the function that writes +0x17=0x80 separately calls SetMode(0x10), not 0x80. New hypothesis: mode 0x10, reached by 3 independent transitions and the default fallback, is a structural match for **Company** (the attract loop's anchor screen) -- recorded at `suspected`. Also surfaced `FUN_80021374`, a second +0x28 read site outside the mapped dispatcher tree, not yet reviewed.
* **Review**: Manually reviewed the remaining 10 call targets from the mode dispatcher tree (mode `0x00`'s 3 submode handlers, mode `0x04`'s 2, mode `0xff`'s 5 confirmed jump-table targets), promoting all to `confidence = manual`. Found mode `0x00` is a 3-step countdown state machine whose last step writes `PTR_DAT_800ac8e8+0x17 = 0x80` (a literal top-level mode constant) — the strongest lead yet on how mode transitions actually happen, though the missing link (something copying `+0x17` into the real mode field `+0x28`) hasn't been found. Also found mode `0xff`'s submode `0x04` (`FUN_8002358c`) calls the real PsyQ kernel function `ResetGraph(1)` and writes `PTR_DAT_800ac8e8[9] = 1`, the same byte `main`'s own reset routine unconditionally re-zeroes every pass — flagged as an open question about which of `main`'s remaining unreviewed per-frame calls actually reads it. 12 new call targets discovered, none yet reviewed.
* **Screen flow**: Recorded the repository owner's domain-knowledge account (former StepMania Team developer, BEMANI genre convention) of the expected boot-to-attract sequence: PS BIOS logo -> Memory Card Auto Load -> Caution -> Attract Loop (Company -> How To Play -> Gameplay Demonstration -> Ranking -> back to Company). Paired mode `0x00` with **Memory Card Auto Load** at `confidence: suspected`, backed by a real structural fact: `FUN_8002216c`'s boot-time state reset zeroes the mode field itself, so mode `0x00` is guaranteed to be the first value the dispatcher ever sees. The remaining five screens are not yet paired to specific mode values — flagged as an open gap pending string/asset evidence in the unreviewed handlers or a full map of `+0x28`'s write sites.
* **Review**: Manually reviewed the 5 smallest handlers discovered by `FUN_80022cf8`'s mode dispatcher (modes `0x00`, `0x04`, `0x20`, `0x32`, `0xff`), promoting them to `confidence = manual`. Corrected two "single call, no args observed" notes: modes `0x00` and `0x04` are themselves submode dispatchers on the same `PTR_DAT_800ac8e8+0x2a` field mode `0x02`/default already use — meaning `+0x2a` is likely a general per-screen phase counter, not scoped to one screen. Also found modes `0x20` and `0x32` have byte-for-byte identical handler bodies, echoing the open "why do `0x10` and default share a dispatch" question. 10 new call targets discovered (3 for mode `0x00`, 2 for mode `0x04`, 5 jump-table targets for mode `0xff`), none yet reviewed.
* **Tooling**: Added `tools/ghidra/scripts/DumpJumpTable.java`, a reusable headless script that reads N consecutive 32-bit words at an address and resolves each to a function name. Used it to read the raw bytes of mode `0xff`'s jump table at `0x8001a840`, confirming the submode-0..4-to-callee mapping inferred earlier the same day from code-layout convention alone.

## 2026-07-13
* **Target selection**: Selected Dance Dance Revolution 5th Mix (Japan, serial `SLPM-86897`) as the project's first target revision with a `byte_match` success contract, decided by the repository owner.

* **Tooling**: Added a PowerShell ISO 9660 reader that resolves `SYSTEM.CNF`, extracts the boot executable, and parses its PS-X EXE header.
* **Analysis**: Installed a portable, git-ignored Ghidra 12.1.2 + JDK 25 + `ghidra_psx_ldr` and imported the boot executable headlessly; its PsyQ Signatures analyzer identified PsyQ SDK `4.4.0` as the original toolchain.
* **Schema**: Added a symbol map schema (CSV columns, confidence tiers) and exported the first tool-generated symbol map for the boot executable (2,026 functions; 1,045 PsyQ-signature-matched, 981 unverified).
* **Review**: Manually reviewed the PsyQ crt0 startup chain (`start`→`stup1`→`InitHeap`→`stup0`→`main`→`trap`), promoting 4 functions to `confidence = manual` and locating `main` at `0x800226a4` as the crt0-to-game-code boundary.
* **Coordination**: Removed the file-based handoff claim/coordination system (`docs/handoffs/`) at the repository owner's direction. Agents now commit and push completed work directly instead of waiting for user review; see `AGENTS.md`'s "Commit and push" section. Configured `origin` to `https://github.com/RieLoveChan/RiePS1`.
* **Review**: Manually reviewed `main` (`0x800226a4`), confirming it is a standard PS1 main loop (subsystem init, RCNT2 vsync/timer IRQ setup via correctly-named PsyQ kernel calls, then an infinite frame loop). All 12 of its unnamed callees matched no PsyQ signature, confirming genuinely DDR-5th-Mix-specific code has now been located and is ready for review.
* **Schema**: Added a quirk-entry schema for recording non-standard engineering practices, with a two-tier structure (per-game log, promoted to a cross-game vendor-quirks log only once a pattern is independently confirmed in a second game). Created the (currently empty) DDR 5th Mix quirk log and the cross-game log.
* **Review**: Manually reviewed `FUN_8002216c`, correcting an earlier hypothesis in `main`'s notes — it is a one-shot state-reset call, not a wait/spin loop condition, because it unconditionally zeroes the exact flag byte its caller's loop tests. Local disk cleanup: removed ~3.08GB of duplicate disc copies across `runtime/bizhawk/runs/*/disc/` (git-ignored, fully reproducible from `input/`), keeping only the small evidence files.
* **Review**: Manually reviewed `FUN_80022cf8` and identified it as the game's mode/screen dispatcher — reads a 16-bit field at `PTR_DAT_800ac8e8+0x28` and branches to ~9 distinct handlers by sparse enum-like values, with a second-level submode dispatch at `+0x2a`. Discovered 14 new unreviewed call targets (mode handlers + a shared per-frame epilogue); this is now the best entry point for mapping the game's top-level screen flow.
* **Schema**: Added a screen/mode flow schema and the DDR 5th Mix screen-flow document, pre-populated with the mode dispatcher's structural facts (handler addresses, sizes, arguments) and empty `proposed screen`/`confidence` columns. The repository owner is a former StepMania Team developer and will fill in domain-informed screen hypotheses for verification against the actual handler code.
